So, yeah, I'm definitely not eating like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I know I feel pretty low right now, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts about doing anything like that to myself. I'm just trying to get through my shifts, really. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how active or sluggish you feel compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sluggish, really. I mean, I'm still on my feet all night for work, moving around the bar. It's not like I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. I just... I don't feel any different in how I move, I guess. So, no, no real change there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A lot, actually. I just... I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders at work. And there were these local artists I promised I'd give some space to in the bar to show their stuff, and I just dropped the ball on all of it. I ruined their shot. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how much I messed that up for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more or less than you used to, like you're restless or fidgety, or if it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, I don't feel like I'm moving any slower, and I wouldn't say I'm restless or fidgety either. I'm just doing my usual routine, working my shifts. They came in and changed everything I had been working toward, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this began.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say empty is probably the right word for it. Sad, maybe... but mostly just empty. It's like I'm just going through the motions every day, and there's just nothing really there inside. So, yeah, I feel like that pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've lost some weight. Most days, honestly, I just forget to eat. I don't even realize it until my hands start shaking when I'm pouring drinks behind the bar. Then, after we close, I might try to grab a few bites of something cold in the back, but... I just feel sick even looking at it. So, yeah, I'm definitely not eating like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I know I feel pretty low right now, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts about doing anything like that to myself. I'm just trying to get through my shifts, really. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how active or sluggish you feel compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sluggish, really. I mean, I'm still on my feet all night for work, moving around the bar. It's not like I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. I just... I don't feel any different in how I move, I guess. So, no, no real change there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A lot, actually. I just... I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders at work. And there were these local artists I promised I'd give some space to in the bar to show their stuff, and I just dropped the ball on all of it. I ruined their shot. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how much I messed that up for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more or less than you used to, like you're restless or fidgety, or if it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, I don't feel like I'm moving any slower, and I wouldn't say I'm restless or fidgety either. I'm just doing my usual routine, working my shifts. It doesn't feel like it takes more effort to move around or anything. It's just the same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it ties into what I was just saying about the guilt. I just feel like I'm completely useless. I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders who look up to me, and those artists I promised wall space to. I completely wrecked their shot at getting seen. It makes me feel like I have no value, like I'm just a failure who ruins things for other people. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I'm just really tired. They came in and changed everything I had been working toward, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this began.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say empty is probably the right word for it. Sad, maybe... but mostly just empty. It's like I'm just going through the motions every day, and there's just nothing really there inside. So, yeah, I feel like that pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've lost some weight. Most days, honestly, I just forget to eat. I don't even realize it until my hands start shaking when I'm pouring drinks behind the bar. Then, after we close, I might try to grab a few bites of something cold in the back, but... I just feel sick even looking at it. So, yeah, I'm definitely not eating like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I know I feel pretty low right now, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts about doing anything like that to myself. I'm just trying to get through my shifts, really. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how active or sluggish you feel compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sluggish, really. I mean, I'm still on my feet all night for work, moving around the bar. It's not like I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. I just... I don't feel any different in how I move, I guess. So, no, no real change there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A lot, actually. I just... I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders at work. And there were these local artists I promised I'd give some space to in the bar to show their stuff, and I just dropped the ball on all of it. I ruined their shot. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how much I messed that up for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more or less than you used to, like you're restless or fidgety, or if it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, I don't feel like I'm moving any slower, and I wouldn't say I'm restless or fidgety either. I'm just doing my usual routine, working my shifts. It doesn't feel like it takes more effort to move around or anything. It's just the same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it ties into what I was just saying about the guilt. I just feel like I'm completely useless. I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders who look up to me, and those artists I promised wall space to. I completely wrecked their shot at getting seen. It makes me feel like I have no value, like I'm just a failure who ruins things for other people. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I'm just really tired. Like, by the time I carry a couple cases of liquor from the back room, or just set up the well for my shift... my body feels like I've already worked a double. And that's even if I barely did anything at all that day. So, yeah, it definitely takes a lot of effort just to get through the basics.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing more or fewer activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I'm pretty much doing the same amount of things. Just going to work, doing my shifts, coming home. I haven't really picked up anything new, but I haven't stopped doing my regular routine either. It's just about the same, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad at work. Doing inventory used to be totally automatic for me, just second nature. But lately, I'll just stand there staring at the bottles, and I'll lose count like three times in a row. And making decisions... even something stupid simple, like trying to figure out which garnish I need to restock for the night, I just freeze up. I can't seem to process it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because nothing really feels like mine anymore. I used to get excited about things, you know? Like checking out murals around town, going for runs, watching basketball... even just making a really good drink at work. But lately, I just stand there behind the bar, and I realize I just don't care about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, the pleasure is pretty much gone. I don't enjoy those things at all anymore. Whether it's watching basketball, going for a run, or just mixing a drink at work, the feeling just isn't there. Like I said, I just don't care about any of it now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about two months now. It started right around the time the bar got bought out. They came in and changed everything I had been working toward, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this began.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say empty is probably the right word for it. Sad, maybe... but mostly just empty. It's like I'm just going through the motions every day, and there's just nothing really there inside. So, yeah, I feel like that pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've lost some weight. Most days, honestly, I just forget to eat. I don't even realize it until my hands start shaking when I'm pouring drinks behind the bar. Then, after we close, I might try to grab a few bites of something cold in the back, but... I just feel sick even looking at it. So, yeah, I'm definitely not eating like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I know I feel pretty low right now, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts about doing anything like that to myself. I'm just trying to get through my shifts, really. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how active or sluggish you feel compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sluggish, really. I mean, I'm still on my feet all night for work, moving around the bar. It's not like I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. I just... I don't feel any different in how I move, I guess. So, no, no real change there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A lot, actually. I just... I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders at work. And there were these local artists I promised I'd give some space to in the bar to show their stuff, and I just dropped the ball on all of it. I ruined their shot. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how much I messed that up for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more or less than you used to, like you're restless or fidgety, or if it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, I don't feel like I'm moving any slower, and I wouldn't say I'm restless or fidgety either. I'm just doing my usual routine, working my shifts. It doesn't feel like it takes more effort to move around or anything. It's just the same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it ties into what I was just saying about the guilt. I just feel like I'm completely useless. I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders who look up to me, and those artists I promised wall space to. I completely wrecked their shot at getting seen. It makes me feel like I have no value, like I'm just a failure who ruins things for other people. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I'm just really tired. Like, by the time I carry a couple cases of liquor from the back room, or just set up the well for my shift... my body feels like I've already worked a double. And that's even if I barely did anything at all that day. So, yeah, it definitely takes a lot of effort just to get through the basics.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing more or fewer activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I'm pretty much doing the same amount of things. Just going to work, doing my shifts, coming home. I haven't really picked up anything new, but I haven't stopped doing my regular routine either. It's just about the same, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad at work. Doing inventory used to be totally automatic for me, just second nature. But lately, I'll just stand there staring at the bottles, and I'll lose count like three times in a row. And making decisions... even something stupid simple, like trying to figure out which garnish I need to restock for the night, I just freeze up. I can't seem to process it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure what you mean by that. I haven't noticed anything like pacing around or feeling super restless, if that's what you're asking. And I don't think I'm moving in slow motion or anything. I'm just tired and dragging, you know? But physically, I feel like I'm moving about the same as usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you wished you were dead or wanted to fall asleep and not wake up since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I'm definitely exhausted, but I don't want to die or anything. I just want to get back to feeling like myself and get through my shifts without it being so hard. So, no, I haven't wished I wouldn't wake up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But lately, I just stand there behind the bar, and I realize I just don't care about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, the pleasure is pretty much gone. I don't enjoy those things at all anymore. Whether it's watching basketball, going for a run, or just mixing a drink at work, the feeling just isn't there. Like I said, I just don't care about any of it now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about two months now. It started right around the time the bar got bought out. They came in and changed everything I had been working toward, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this began.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say empty is probably the right word for it. Sad, maybe... but mostly just empty. It's like I'm just going through the motions every day, and there's just nothing really there inside. So, yeah, I feel like that pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've lost some weight. Most days, honestly, I just forget to eat. I don't even realize it until my hands start shaking when I'm pouring drinks behind the bar. Then, after we close, I might try to grab a few bites of something cold in the back, but... I just feel sick even looking at it. So, yeah, I'm definitely not eating like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I know I feel pretty low right now, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts about doing anything like that to myself. I'm just trying to get through my shifts, really. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how active or sluggish you feel compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sluggish, really. It doesn't feel like it takes more effort to move around or anything. It's just the same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it ties into what I was just saying about the guilt. I just feel like I'm completely useless. I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders who look up to me, and those artists I promised wall space to. I completely wrecked their shot at getting seen. It makes me feel like I have no value, like I'm just a failure who ruins things for other people. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I'm just really tired. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things. I mean, I haven't completely stopped everything, but... I've missed several shifts at the bar. And when I am there, I've been making mistakes during closing that I never used to make. It's embarrassing, honestly. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because nothing really feels like mine anymore. I used to get excited about things, you know? Like checking out murals around town, going for runs, watching basketball... even just making a really good drink at work. But lately, I just stand there behind the bar, and I realize I just don't care about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, the pleasure is pretty much gone. I don't enjoy those things at all anymore. Whether it's watching basketball, going for a run, or just mixing a drink at work, the feeling just isn't there. Like I said, I just don't care about any of it now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about two months now. It started right around the time the bar got bought out. They came in and changed everything I had been working toward, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this began.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say empty is probably the right word for it. Sad, maybe... but mostly just empty. It's like I'm just going through the motions every day, and there's just nothing really there inside. So, yeah, I feel like that pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've lost some weight. Most days, honestly, I just forget to eat. I don't even realize it until my hands start shaking when I'm pouring drinks behind the bar. Then, after we close, I might try to grab a few bites of something cold in the back, but... I just feel sick even looking at it. So, yeah, I'm definitely not eating like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I know I feel pretty low right now, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts about doing anything like that to myself. I'm just trying to get through my shifts, really. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how active or sluggish you feel compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sluggish, really. I mean, I'm still on my feet all night for work, moving around the bar. It's not like I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. I just... I don't feel any different in how I move, I guess. So, no, no real change there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A lot, actually. I just... I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders at work. And there were these local artists I promised I'd give some space to in the bar to show their stuff, and I just dropped the ball on all of it. I ruined their shot. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how much I messed that up for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more or less than you used to, like you're restless or fidgety, or if it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, I don't feel like I'm moving any slower, and I wouldn't say I'm restless or fidgety either. I'm just doing my usual routine, working my shifts. It doesn't feel like it takes more effort to move around or anything. It's just the same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it ties into what I was just saying about the guilt. I just feel like I'm completely useless. I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders who look up to me, and those artists I promised wall space to. I completely wrecked their shot at getting seen. It makes me feel like I have no value, like I'm just a failure who ruins things for other people. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I'm just really tired. Like, by the time I carry a couple cases of liquor from the back room, or just set up the well for my shift... my body feels like I've already worked a double. And that's even if I barely did anything at all that day. So, yeah, it definitely takes a lot of effort just to get through the basics.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing more or fewer activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I'm pretty much doing the same amount of things. Just going to work, doing my shifts, coming home. I haven't really picked up anything new, but I haven't stopped doing my regular routine either. It's just about the same, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad at work. Doing inventory used to be totally automatic for me, just second nature. But lately, I'll just stand there staring at the bottles, and I'll lose count like three times in a row. And making decisions... even something stupid simple, like trying to figure out which garnish I need to restock for the night, I just freeze up. I can't seem to process it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure what you mean by that. I haven't noticed anything like pacing around or feeling super restless, if that's what you're asking. And I don't think I'm moving in slow motion or anything. I'm just tired and dragging, you know? But physically, I feel like I'm moving about the same as usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you wished you were dead or wanted to fall asleep and not wake up since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I'm definitely exhausted, but I don't want to die or anything. I just want to get back to feeling like myself and get through my shifts without it being so hard. So, no, I haven't wished I wouldn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling slightly better in the last two months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It's been pretty constant. This whole thing has been going on for about two months straight now. It started right around the time the bar was bought out and... well, everything I had been working toward just got changed. So, no, there haven't really been any breaks in it since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were so severe that you were unable to carry out your daily activities at all?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things. I mean, I haven't completely stopped everything, but... I've missed several shifts at the bar. And when I am there, I've been making mistakes during closing that I never used to make. It's embarrassing, honestly. Now my coworkers have to double-check my work, and they used to just trust me to get it right. \n\nOutside of work, it's bad too. I used to meet up with friends for runs or to play basketball, or go shoot photos of murals around the city. But I've just stopped seeing them. I can't seem to make myself show up for any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about ending your life, even if you don't want to die?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I mentioned earlier, I really don't have any thoughts like that. I haven't thought about ending my life at all. I know things are rough right now, but it's just not something that crosses my mind. I really just want to figure out how to get back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the last two months where you felt even slightly better than the others?\n\nPatient: No, like I said before, it's just been pretty much the same. I haven't really had any good days or days where I felt lighter or anything like that. It's just been a constant heavy feeling for the last couple of months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Like checking out murals around town, going for runs, watching basketball... even just making a really good drink at work. But lately, I just stand there behind the bar, and I realize I just don't care about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just saying, the pleasure is pretty much gone. I don't enjoy those things at all anymore. Whether it's watching basketball, going for a run, or just mixing a drink at work, the feeling just isn't there. Like I said, I just don't care about any of it now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about two months now. It started right around the time the bar got bought out. They came in and changed everything I had been working toward, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this began.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say empty is probably the right word for it. Sad, maybe... but mostly just empty. It's like I'm just going through the motions every day, and there's just nothing really there inside. So, yeah, I feel like that pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've lost some weight. Most days, honestly, I just forget to eat. I don't even realize it until my hands start shaking when I'm pouring drinks behind the bar. Then, after we close, I might try to grab a few bites of something cold in the back, but... I just feel sick even looking at it. So, yeah, I'm definitely not eating like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I know I feel pretty low right now, but... no, I haven't had any thoughts about doing anything like that to myself. I'm just trying to get through my shifts, really. So, no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how active or sluggish you feel compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sluggish, really. I mean, I'm still on my feet all night for work, moving around the bar. It's not like I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. I just... I don't feel any different in how I move, I guess. So, no, no real change there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. A lot, actually. I just... I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders at work. And there were these local artists I promised I'd give some space to in the bar to show their stuff, and I just dropped the ball on all of it. I ruined their shot. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me because of how much I messed that up for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more or less than you used to, like you're restless or fidgety, or if it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said before, I don't feel like I'm moving any slower, and I wouldn't say I'm restless or fidgety either. I'm just doing my usual routine, working my shifts. It doesn't feel like it takes more effort to move around or anything. It's just the same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it ties into what I was just saying about the guilt. I just feel like I'm completely useless. I keep thinking about how I let down the younger bartenders who look up to me, and those artists I promised wall space to. I completely wrecked their shot at getting seen. It makes me feel like I have no value, like I'm just a failure who ruins things for other people. Honestly, I feel like I deserve whatever happens to me because of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like it's an effort to do even small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I'm just really tired. Like, by the time I carry a couple cases of liquor from the back room, or just set up the well for my shift... my body feels like I've already worked a double. And that's even if I barely did anything at all that day. So, yeah, it definitely takes a lot of effort just to get through the basics.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing more or fewer activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I'm pretty much doing the same amount of things. Just going to work, doing my shifts, coming home. I haven't really picked up anything new, but I haven't stopped doing my regular routine either. It's just about the same, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad at work. Doing inventory used to be totally automatic for me, just second nature. But lately, I'll just stand there staring at the bottles, and I'll lose count like three times in a row. And making decisions... even something stupid simple, like trying to figure out which garnish I need to restock for the night, I just freeze up. I can't seem to process it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure what you mean by that. I haven't noticed anything like pacing around or feeling super restless, if that's what you're asking. And I don't think I'm moving in slow motion or anything. I'm just tired and dragging, you know? But physically, I feel like I'm moving about the same as usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you wished you were dead or wanted to fall asleep and not wake up since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I'm definitely exhausted, but I don't want to die or anything. I just want to get back to feeling like myself and get through my shifts without it being so hard. So, no, I haven't wished I wouldn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling slightly better in the last two months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It's been pretty constant. This whole thing has been going on for about two months straight now. It started right around the time the bar was bought out and... well, everything I had been working toward just got changed. So, no, there haven't really been any breaks in it since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were so severe that you were unable to carry out your daily activities at all?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things. I mean, I haven't completely stopped everything, but... I've missed several shifts at the bar. And when I am there, I've been making mistakes during closing that I never used to make. It's embarrassing, honestly. Now my coworkers have to double-check my work, and they used to just trust me to get it right. \n\nOutside of work, it's bad too. I used to meet up with friends for runs or to play basketball, or go shoot photos of murals around the city. But I've just stopped seeing them. I can't seem to make myself show up for any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about ending your life, even if you don't want to die?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I mentioned earlier, I really don't have any thoughts like that. I haven't thought about ending my life at all. I know things are rough right now, but it's just not something that crosses my mind. I really just want to figure out how to get back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the last two months where you felt even slightly better than the others?\n\nPatient: No, like I said before, it's just been pretty much the same. I haven't really had any good days or days where I felt lighter or anything like that. It's just been a constant heavy feeling for the last couple of months.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were so severe that you were unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had that happen. I mean... it can be really tough to get going most mornings, but I always manage to get up eventually. I haven't been completely stuck in bed all day or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
